### Recently-shipped rows archived by the PR #924 fill (5-row cap)

| [#909](https://github.com/Kelsean868/agencytrack/pull/909) | `ee5444bd` | **Daily Capture call fields — the type split, `serviceCalls`, `referralsObtained`.** `DAILY_ACTIVITY_VERSION` 1 → 2, **no migration**: the split is ADDITIVE — `dials` stays the authoritative daily total and a new optional `dialsByType {cold, referral, followUp, seminarTradeshow}` rides alongside it, so every pre-v2 daily doc aggregates to **byte-identical** weekly numbers (evidenced in the PR: `coldCalls = Σdials = 30`, three siblings 0, before and after). The aggregator fallback is decided **PER ENTRY, not per week** — that is what preserves the total in a mixed week where some days carry a breakdown and others do not; a per-week fallback would silently drop the unsplit days. **Points-neutral by construction** (`computePoints` sums the four dial types then floors ONCE), asserted by a `fast-check` property at `numRuns` 200 / seed `20260826` and **mutation-verified** — dropping the fallback fails both properties. **Ruling D-SC closes a live defect (F3):** the daily pace badge and the aggregated weekly draft scored the same week differently, because `computeDayPoints` mapped `serviceContacts → serviceCalls` while the aggregators wrote no `serviceCalls` at all. Both now read a REAL daily `serviceCalls` field — an attempt, distinct from `serviceContacts`, a reach. ⚠ **Deliberate correction, not a regression:** a day carrying only `serviceContacts` now scores **0** for service (it scored 1pt each), and there is **no UI to enter `serviceCalls`** — the KQM Calls ingest endpoint populates it, and this slice does not build that endpoint. `serviceCalls` stays excluded from every funnel sum (`funnelModel.js`) and every plan/effort sum (`planVariance.js`); the "count converted service calls as Tel Contacts" FU is **untouched and still open** — D-SC ruled on where the number comes from, not what it counts toward. **Ruling D-TD:** "Target Dials" means the **4-sum**, read through `extractFields.totalTelAttempts` rather than re-summed inline, so a fifth call type can never create a third definition of the same total; closes the Wizard-v3 Target-Dials FU. **Deploy-gated:** `aggregateDailyToWeekly` lives in `functions/` — merging ships the code, NOT the cron. `firebase deploy --only functions` is a separate dispatcher action (Rule 19) and is NOT done by this PR. |


### 'Where we left off' block archived by the PR #924 fill (Rule 16 cap 3)

---

**PRIOR — 2026-08-26. PR #915 merged to `main` (squash `302b87a6`) — linked call sources slice A: the link and its lifecycle. MERGED AND DEPLOYED, LIVE IN PRODUCTION** — superseded same-day by #919 above, before any smoke ran; read as history of how the self-service model was arrived at, not as the current model.

A new collection, `tenants/{tid}/callSources/{sourceId}`, that lets an external calling system present a bearer token which resolves to a stored link naming the agent whose KPIs a call should move — identity never travels in the request body, so a typo or tampered field can't silently credit the wrong agent. This slice is deliberately narrow: create, list, revoke, and revoke-on-deactivation. No ingest endpoint yet — nothing reads the token.

The load-bearing design decision is the **F4 trap**: `firestore.rules`' existing `canManage()` routes through `isManager()`, which includes `unit_manager`, and a unit manager must never be able to mint a token that writes another agent's KPIs. Both the callables and a new, deliberately narrower `canManageCallSources()` rules helper enforce this independently, and both were mutation-verified — swapping in `canManage()` was tried on purpose and caught by exactly the tests built to catch it, nothing else.

**CodeRabbit, triggered explicitly (Ruling 19), found four real defects in code this PR wrote, all fixed before merge.** The one worth remembering: the new rules helper was written by copying `canManage()`'s shape, which bundles `tenant_admin` with `platform_admin` as both cross-tenant. That's correct for `platform_admin` (lives outside any tenant) but wrong for `tenant_admin` (CLAUDE.md: "all access within a single tenant") — on a collection of *credentials*, a cross-tenant read is a credential leak, not just an over-broad view. Worth a beat of thought next time a new rules helper gets modelled on an existing one: copying the shape can copy the shape's own scoping mistake.

**CORRECTED 2026-08-26 (post-#919 fill) — this WAS deployed, at 18:25 UTC the same day, then superseded before any smoke ran.** This PR modified the existing `deactivateUser` CF, which put the rules+functions deploy at post-merge only per the additive-carve-out rule; `firebase deploy --only functions,firestore:rules` ran as that dispatcher action (Rule 19). The staging smoke never happened — before it could, the operator ruled the manager-scoped model wrong entirely and #919 replaced it same-day. Read the deploy details above as accurate and this paragraph's original "none of this exists" claim as stale the moment #919 merged.

One CI wrinkle: GitHub created no workflow run at all for two consecutive pushes (~15 minutes each), despite the workflow having no path filters — the same silent-no-run condition banked from v3 P0-D. It self-resolved before merge (checks eventually reported SUCCESS on the final head) and was not investigated further.

Next: the deploy, then the staging smoke. **Next for v3: P0-F** (task↔event unification), unaffected by this slice.

## Archived by the PR #926 post-merge fill (2026-08-27, Rule 16 caps)

- **PR #919 merged to `main` (squash `f11aa505`, 2026-08-26) — call sources become self-service, slice A-prime: creation is self-credit only.** Same day as slice A shipped, the operator ruled that a **manager creating an agent's link** was wrong — each agent attaches their own calling software, no manager in the loop. This wasn't a taste call: slice A's `creditUid`-in-the-payload shape was the exact mechanism behind **both** of its security defects (the pre-build `unit_manager` trap and the CodeRabbit-caught tenant-isolation hole). Self-credit deletes the class rather than patching instances — `creditUid` is now set server-side from the verified token, and **presence** of `creditUid` in the payload is itself rejected (`invalid-argument`), even when it names the caller, so the field cannot survive in anyone's mental model of the API. The role gate is **removed, not widened** — there is no privilege to check because there is no cross-user effect. Rules read is now owner-only (`creditUid == request.auth.uid`, no role anywhere in the clause); `canManageCallSources` is **deleted** as dead code. `revokeCallSource` answers non-ownership with `not-found` rather than `permission-denied`, so a prober cannot confirm an id exists. **The load-bearing test — a caller cannot credit anyone but themselves — is mutation-verified:** restoring slice A's payload-reads-creditUid path turned exactly those three tests red, nothing else. The tab moved off `ManagerDashboard` onto `AgentDashboard` (`src/components/admin/` → `src/components/callSources/`, since it's agent-facing now). **Delegation is unchanged:** an agent attaches their assistant's calling-software profile, crediting themselves; the assistant needs no AgencyTrack account. **DEPLOYED 2026-08-26 20:57 UTC — LIVE IN PRODUCTION.** `firebase deploy --only functions,firestore:rules` ran as the Rule 19 dispatcher action minutes after the merge: `firestore: released rules firestore.rules to cloud.firestore` (the `released` line, not merely `compiled`), 28 of 28 functions updated, exit 0. **Verified independently of the CLI's own summary** — `gcloud functions describe` reports `createCallSource` `updateTime` `2026-08-26T20:56:58.773Z` ACTIVE and `revokeCallSource` `2026-08-26T20:56:53.393Z` ACTIVE, both after the squash. Production now enforces the self-service model. Four functions (`setAgentOfMonth`, `validateKioskToken`, `resolveSalesManagerUid`, `sendMondayNudge`) emitted `failed to update` mid-run and **all four then succeeded on the CLI's own retry** — the warnings are never withdrawn, so the log reads worse than the outcome; nothing was half-deployed, and that was checked rather than assumed. ⚠ **This entry was originally stamped `NOT YET DEPLOYED` by #919's own post-merge fill (`88a5b68a`), which was written AFTER the deploy had already run** — the third consecutive cycle with that exact error (#909→#910, #915→#916, #919→here). Corrected, and the mechanism removed rather than the instance patched: `/post-merge` now carries a step 5a requiring deploy state to be QUERIED from Firebase, never inferred from the filling session's own inaction. **A CodeRabbit gap surfaced here, not a code defect — and it is a same-day DOWNGRADE, not a standing condition.** #919's own post-merge fill described the Free plan as though it had always been so and dated slice A's review "six days ago"; both are wrong, and the corrected facts are actionable where the original framing was not. From the run-configuration blocks on the two PRs: slice A (#915) was reviewed **2026-08-26 15:09 UTC on `Plan: Pro Plus`** and produced **5 inline findings, 4 of them real defects**; #919 was reviewed **2026-08-26 20:30 UTC on `Plan: Free`** and produced **0 inline findings** — same repo, same `CHILL` profile, **5.4 hours apart on the same day**. The Free entitlement also states "up to 1 included review per hour". So the second-reviewer slot did not fall silent; **the plan dropped from Pro Plus to Free mid-afternoon on 2026-08-26**, and the Rule 21 backstop is degraded until billing is restored. Operator action, not a code follow-up. **A known gap left open:** a producing manager (UM/BM) is now permitted by the callable to attach their own source but has no UI to do it from — permitted-but-unreachable, the same shape CodeRabbit caught on slice A, mirrored rather than fixed, because fixing it means re-adding a nav entry this brief explicitly told the slice to remove; banked MEDIUM pending an operator ruling on whether a producing manager counts as "an agent" here. Brief: `docs/briefs/call-sources-self-service-kickoff.md`.

- `f11aa505` (PR #919 squash — call sources become self-service, slice A-prime, 2026-08-26). **MERGED *AND* DEPLOYED — `firebase deploy --only functions,firestore:rules` ran 2026-08-26 20:57 UTC.** `firestore: released rules firestore.rules to cloud.firestore`, 28/28 functions updated, exit 0; `gcloud functions describe` independently confirms `createCallSource` (`updateTime` `2026-08-26T20:56:58.773Z`, ACTIVE) and `revokeCallSource` (`2026-08-26T20:56:53.393Z`, ACTIVE). **Production serves the self-service model:** a client hitting `createCallSource` now has no role gate to satisfy and is hard-rejected if it sends `creditUid` at all. Four unrelated functions logged `failed to update` mid-run and all four succeeded on retry — no half-deploy. **This field said `MERGED, NOT DEPLOYED` until PR #920 corrected it**; the #919 fill inferred that from its own inaction while the deploy had already run. See the note in `Last updated` and `/post-merge` step 5a.

- **Call sources become self-service, slice A-prime merged (PR #919, `f11aa505`) — MERGED AND DEPLOYED, LIVE IN PRODUCTION 2026-08-26 20:57 UTC.** Same day slice A shipped, the operator ruled managers out of the loop entirely: each agent attaches their own calling software, self-credit only. **The deploy gate is CLEARED** — rules released, `createCallSource`/`revokeCallSource` both ACTIVE with post-squash `updateTime`s, verified via `gcloud functions describe` rather than the deploy log alone. **NEXT ACTION: the smoke** — sign in as an ordinary agent, attach a source, see it listed, revoke it. B retired the `serviceCalls` half only. **v3 Phase 0 and the Daily Capture call-fields track are both unchanged by this PR.**


### 'Where we left off' block archived by the PR #926 fill (Rule 16 cap 3)

---

**PRIOR — 2026-08-26. PR #919 merged to `main` (squash `f11aa505`) — call sources become self-service, slice A-prime. MERGED AND DEPLOYED, LIVE IN PRODUCTION.**

Same day slice A (#915) shipped, the operator ruled managers out of the loop entirely: each agent attaches their own calling software, self-credit only, no manager in between. Worth stating why this isn't a taste reversal — slice A's `creditUid`-in-the-payload shape was the exact mechanism behind **both** of its security defects (the `unit_manager` trap caught before build, the tenant-isolation hole CodeRabbit caught in review). Forcing self-credit doesn't patch either instance; it removes the class, because there is no longer a "someone else" to name.

`creditUid` is now set server-side from the verified token. Its mere **presence** in the payload — even naming the caller themselves — is a hard `invalid-argument` rejection, deliberately: a client that can send a field harmlessly today is a client that ships the wrong assumption tomorrow. The role gate is gone entirely (nothing to check when there's no cross-user effect), the rules' read arm is owner-only with no role anywhere in it, and `canManageCallSources` — which existed only to serve the manager read — is deleted rather than left as dead code shaped like a security gate.

**The load-bearing test is mutation-verified**, same discipline as slice A: restoring the old payload-reads-creditUid path turned exactly the three cross-credit tests red, nothing else.

**Deployed, and verified rather than assumed.** `firebase deploy --only functions,firestore:rules` ran at 20:57 UTC, minutes after the merge: `released rules firestore.rules to cloud.firestore` (the `released` line — `compiled successfully` alone would not have been a deploy), 28 of 28 functions updated, exit 0. Because the log emitted four `failed to update` warnings mid-run and never withdrew them once the CLI's own retry succeeded, the outcome was confirmed against Google directly: `gcloud functions describe` puts `createCallSource` at `2026-08-26T20:56:58.773Z` ACTIVE and `revokeCallSource` at `2026-08-26T20:56:53.393Z` ACTIVE. Production enforces self-credit.

**The smoke is now the only thing standing between this and done, and it is owed twice.** Slice A never got one before being superseded; this PR inherited the debt and adds its own. Nothing else in this track should start first.

⚠ **This section originally read "Not deployed", and that is the third cycle running it has been wrong** (#909→#910, #915→#916, now #919→#920). The mechanism was always the same: the post-merge fill is written by a session that did not itself deploy, and it reasons from its own inaction to a claim about production. The fix is not another correction PR — it is `/post-merge` step 5a, added in #920, which requires deploy state to be QUERIED from Firebase. **Nothing in git knows what Firebase is serving.**

**A gap worth flagging rather than code — and it is a downgrade, not a standing condition.** CodeRabbit reviewed #915 at 15:09 UTC on **Pro Plus** and returned five inline findings, four of them real defects; it reviewed #919 at 20:30 UTC on **Free** and returned a walkthrough with none. Same repo, same `CHILL` profile, 5.4 hours apart the same day — not "six days ago", as the original fill said. The Free tier also caps at one included review per hour. So the second-reviewer slot did not go quiet; the plan dropped mid-afternoon, and the Rule 21 backstop stays degraded on exactly the kind of PR — auth model, credentials collection — where it had just proved its value. Restoring billing is an operator action.

**One known gap left open, not fixed:** a producing manager (UM/BM) is now permitted by the callable to attach their own source (no role gate) but has no UI to reach it from, since the tab moved fully to `AgentDashboard`. Permitted-but-unreachable is the same shape CodeRabbit caught on #915, mirrored rather than closed — fixing it means re-adding a nav entry this brief explicitly said to remove, and the operator's ruling doesn't say whether a producing manager counts as "an agent" here. Banked MEDIUM, one-line fix written down for whenever that's decided.

Next: **the smoke against *this* model** — the deploy is done. **Next for v3: P0-F**, unaffected.

## Archived by the post-merge fill for PR #927 (2026-08-27)

Rule 16 caps: `Recently shipped` 5 rows; the top-table fields and `Where we left off` 3 entries each. Moved verbatim.

### Last updated - overflow

**PR #923 merged to `main` (squash `0a6d8e9c`, 2026-08-26) - `ingestCallActivity`, slice B: the endpoint that consumes the token.** The first code anywhere that writes an agent's KPIs from OUTSIDE AgencyTrack. One POST per call: bearer token in, one outcome in, the token resolves to a `callSources` doc, that doc's `creditUid` says whose numbers move, the outcome maps through ONE table, the day's `dailyActivity` is bumped. **Identity comes from the token, never the payload** - a body carrying `creditUid`/`agentEmail`/`uid`/`tenantId` is a hard 400, the same delete-the-class move A-prime made on creation. Three shapes make defect classes impossible rather than caught: `dialsByType` is a PARTITION (property-tested, 300 sequences, seed `20260826`); idempotency lives INSIDE the transaction on `sourceApp + sourceId`; every KPI moves via `FieldValue.increment`. **The second mutation is the lesson:** moving the idempotency check OUTSIDE the transaction leaves both SEQUENTIAL replay tests GREEN and reds only the two concurrent ones - a suite without the concurrent variant would have certified a double-counting endpoint. **A real bug caught before any test was written:** Firestore's `set(..., {merge:true})` does not read dotted paths as nested (only `update()` does), so `dialsByType.cold` would have become a literal top-level key - plausible in the console, invisible to the aggregator, every call-type number silently zero forever. **DEPLOYED 2026-08-27 02:01 UTC** - `Successful create operation`, exit 0; `gcloud functions describe` confirms `ingestCallActivity` ACTIVE at `02:01:05.698Z` and `aggregateDailyToWeekly` at `02:01:06.374Z`, both post-squash. A live probe returns an **indistinguishable HTTP 401** for no-token / garbage-token / malformed-header, so the endpoint is not a token-enumeration oracle. **Still owed: the SMOKE, not the deploy** - the endpoint has never completed a successful WRITE against a real Firestore, so idempotency rests on a transaction fake until it does (banked HIGH). Brief: `docs/briefs/ingest-call-activity-kickoff.md`.

A CI anomaly — no workflow run at all on two consecutive pushes despite no path filters — self-resolved before merge. |

### Where we left off - overflow

**PRIOR — 2026-08-26. PR #923 merged to `main` (squash `0a6d8e9c`) — `ingestCallActivity`, slice B: the endpoint that consumes the token. MERGED AND DEPLOYED, LIVE IN PRODUCTION.**

Slices A and A-prime built the link and made it self-service. B is the first code that *reads* the token — and the first anywhere that writes an agent's KPIs from outside AgencyTrack. One POST per call: a bearer token, one outcome. The token resolves to a `callSources` doc, that doc's `creditUid` says whose numbers move, the outcome maps through one table, and the day's `dailyActivity` is bumped. Identity comes from the token and never the payload; a body carrying `creditUid`, `agentEmail`, `uid` or `tenantId` is a hard 400 rather than an ignored field.

**Three shapes were chosen so a defect class cannot occur, rather than so it gets caught.** `dialsByType` is a partition, not a set of tags, so "which bucket" always has exactly one answer. Idempotency lives *inside* the transaction keyed on `sourceApp + sourceId`, not beside it. Every KPI moves via `FieldValue.increment`, never read-modify-write.

**The second mutation test is the one worth remembering.** Deleting the idempotency guard reds exactly the four idempotency tests. But moving the check *outside* the transaction — the plausible check-then-write shape — leaves both **sequential** replay tests green and reds only the two concurrent ones. A suite without the concurrent variant would have certified a double-counting endpoint as correct.

**A real bug was caught before any test existed.** Firestore's `set(..., {merge:true})` does not interpret dotted paths as nested — only `update()` does. `'dialsByType.cold'` would have become a literal top-level key containing a dot: no error, a plausible-looking console, and an aggregator reading `entry.dialsByType.cold` that never sees it. Every call-type number would have sat at zero silently. Fixed with `setNested`.

**Deployed 2026-08-27 02:01 UTC**, exit 0, `Successful create operation` — and verified against Google rather than the deploy log, which emits `failed to update` warnings it never withdraws. `ingestCallActivity` ACTIVE at `02:01:05.698Z`, `aggregateDailyToWeekly` at `02:01:06.374Z`, both post-squash. A live probe returns an indistinguishable HTTP 401 for no token, a garbage token and a malformed header, so the endpoint is not a token-enumeration oracle.

**Watch the two halves.** Only the endpoint was deploy-gated. The aggregator half runs client-side on the Daily Capture save path (`loggingModeService.js:80` ← `DailyCaptureV2.jsx:788`), so the `serviceCalls` guard retirement went live with the Vercel rebuild at merge, no `firebase deploy` involved. That half was smoked before merge and negative-controlled.

**Owed at the time: the smoke, not the deploy — since PAID.** As written, the endpoint had never completed a successful *write* against a real Firestore and its idempotency rested on a transaction fake. PR #924 (`365252dc`, 2026-08-27) closed that: 12 PASS / 0 FAIL against staging, `concurrent-idempotent-live` included. See the CURRENT block above.

Next: the endpoint staging smoke, then slice C — the Supabase webhook on `crm.activities` in `C:\Projects\kqm-crm`, which is what makes calls actually flow. **Slice D got more urgent, not less:** there is no `serviceCalls` input in Daily Capture, only in the weekly wizard, so with the guard retired a typed weekly value is zeroed by the next daily save for every agent until a daily stepper exists.

### Where-we-left-off entry (dropped per the 3-entry cap)

**PRIOR — 2026-08-27. PR #924 merged to `main` (squash `365252dc`) — ingest `TENANT_ID` configurable, and slice B PROVEN against a real Firestore. MERGED AND DEPLOYED TO BOTH PROJECTS.**

The staging smoke is green: **12 PASS / 0 FAIL**. That debt opened with slice A on the 26th, was inherited by A-prime when it superseded A, then carried by B. It is paid.

Two legs close it, and only one of them could ever have been faked:

```
idempotent-live             replay did not double-count (dials still 9)
concurrent-idempotent-live  two simultaneous deliveries moved dials once (9->10); 200/200
```

Until this run, idempotency rested entirely on a transaction fake. The fake models optimistic concurrency properly and was worth building — but it is a model of Firestore, not Firestore, and the concurrent leg is precisely the one that reds a check-then-write implementation. Firestore has now adjudicated it. Also proven live: one call moves `dials`, the mapped bucket and `telContacts` by exactly one; an unknown outcome is a loud 400 rather than a silent zero; `creditUid` in the payload is refused rather than ignored.

**The smoke could not run at all until a constant stopped being a constant, and the mis-rating is the part to remember.** `TENANT_ID` was hardcoded to `tatillife_south`. Staging's tenant is `staging_test`, so a token minted through the staging UI resolved against a tenant absent from that project and 401'd indistinguishably from a forgery. It had been banked MEDIUM as *"a second tenant's tokens would 401 — failing closed, but silently"*, which reads like a multi-tenancy nicety to get to later. The real consequence was that **the endpoint could not be exercised anywhere except production** — which made slice B's own named deliverable impossible by construction. A constant that puts a feature beyond testing outside production is not a deferred nicety, and "fails closed silently" is exactly the phrasing that hid it.

**Three harness errors, all mine, all caught by running rather than reasoning.** The `.gitignore` negation first landed on `.env.*.local` and sat *before* the real `.env.*` rule — gitignore's last match wins, so it did nothing; undetected, the next staging deploy would have silently used the production tenant, the very failure being fixed. The smoke first POSTed from inside the browser and died on CORS, which was the harness modelling a client this server-to-server endpoint does not have; adding CORS to make a test pass would have widened the surface for a caller that doesn't exist. And `partition-holds-live` reported a defect that wasn't one: it asserted `sum(buckets) === dials`, but #909 makes the type split *additive*, so any day mixing hand-entered dials with ingested ones legitimately disagrees. It asserts the delta now — one call, `dials` +1 and exactly one bucket +1 — which is what the endpoint actually guarantees. The stronger version would have gone red on the first real agent's first real day.

**Deployed to both projects**, verified against Google rather than the CLI summary: production `updateTime` `2026-08-27T12:29:06.928Z` ACTIVE, staging `02:25:27.031Z` ACTIVE. Production behaviour is unchanged by construction — the env var is unset there and the default is the constant it replaced, confirmed by the absence of `functions/.env.agencytrack-2a610` and a post-deploy 401 probe.

**Residual, stated rather than implied:** ingest is idempotent but not reversible. The synthetic staging agent's `dials` went 6 → 10 across smoke runs. Fine on a synthetic tenant; not fine against real agents, which is why the smoke refuses any host or project but staging.

Next: **slice C**, the Supabase webhook on `crm.activities` in `C:\Projects\kqm-crm` — the first work in the other repo. ⚠ **CORRECTED BY SLICE C2 (PR #926):** this row said C would be written against `functions/callActivity/outcomeMap.js` "as its contract: 19 outcome codes and 6 campaign codes". That contract matched almost nothing real and was replaced — the endpoint now takes EFFECTS (lane, bucket, four booleans) plus `rawOutcome`/`rawCampaign` for tracing, and holds no KQM vocabulary at all. **Slice D is urgent rather than deferred:** with the guard retired and no `serviceCalls` input in Daily Capture, a typed weekly value is zeroed by the next daily save for every agent.

The shape of the mismatch mattered more than the mismatch: **any design that pins KQM’s words into AgencyTrack’s source stops counting calls the first time an agent adds a campaign.** Operator ruling: **KQM normalises, AgencyTrack validates.** The payload now carries `lane` / `bucket` / `reached` / `booking` / `newName` / `ffi`, and `outcomeMap.js` holds no KQM vocabulary at all — `CALL_OUTCOMES`, `CAMPAIGN_LANES` and `mapCall` are gone (F1 first proved nothing outside the module consumed them). **The contract shrank, so the rejections got STRICTER:** an incoherent effect set is a 400, never a generous reading. **The partition invariant MOVED TO THE BOUNDARY and that is the whole risk of this slice** — `dialsByType` summed to `dials` in B because the campaign was a total function to one bucket; the bucket is now an INPUT, so the sum rests entirely on rejecting `servicing`+bucket and `newBusiness`+null. Closes the LOW outcome×campaign cross-product FU — **by deleting the cross-product, not by restricting it.** |

### Where-we-left-off entry (dropped per the 3-entry cap)

**PRIOR — 2026-08-27. PR #926 merged to `main` (squash `e53a6fc7`) — `ingestCallActivity` accepts EFFECTS, not outcome names. Slice C2. MERGED AND DEPLOYED TO BOTH PROJECTS.**

Scoping slice C turned into a reframing of it. I queried the **live** KQM database (Supabase `oujnscwyxwuzmgyszkxd`) rather than reading the repo's SQL files, and slice B's contract matched almost nothing real:

- **Every campaign code would have 400'd** — the live codes are `schools_2026`, `portfolio_2026`, `referrals_2026`, `group_benefits_2026`, `religious_houses_2026`. The `_2026` suffix alone breaks all five.
- Outcomes arrive as **display labels** (`"No budget - parents pay"`), never as the snake_case keys slice B expected.
- There are **~100 outcome values across 5 campaigns** against slice B's 19, and a trigger seeds a fresh vocabulary for every new campaign.
- **`due_callback` is not a KQM campaign at all** — slice B invented it to carry the follow-up ruling.

**The banked gap had the dependency backwards.** It said slice C must be written against `outcomeMap.js`. But `outcomeMap` was written from the bridge design's table, and that table came from the **schools campaign only** — a fifth of the surface, not the contract. Worth remembering as a shape: a spec derived from one example reads like a spec.

**The ruling: KQM normalises before sending.** Each system owns its own vocabulary, because KQM can create campaigns freely — so any coupled design silently stops counting calls the first time someone adds one. The endpoint now takes `lane`, `bucket`, `reached`, `booking`, `newName`, `ffi`, plus `rawOutcome`/`rawCampaign` **stored for audit and never scored**. `outcomeMap.js` holds no KQM vocabulary at all.

**The partition invariant moved to the boundary.** KQM now computes the bucket, so a servicing call carrying one — or a new-business call missing one — is a hard 400. Trusting the other system to get it right is how a partition quietly becomes a set of tags.

**Both idempotency mutations were re-run, with observed counts.** Deleting the guard reds exactly the four idempotency tests; moving the check outside the transaction leaves both *sequential* replays green and reds only the two concurrent ones. That second mutation is what proves the concurrent test still earns its place after a contract rewrite — which is precisely where safety leaks out.

**Staging smoke: 16 PASS / 0 FAIL against the live endpoint**, re-run independently rather than taken on report. It includes a `retired-contract-rejected` leg that posts the *old* shape and requires a named 400 — so the smoke proves **which build is live** and cannot pass against a stale deployment.

**Deployed to both projects, and the two timestamps are not equivalent.** Production is ACTIVE at `14:38:37.698Z`, deployed *after* the merge, post-squash. Staging is ACTIVE at `14:01:40.608Z` — deployed from branch head `6ff1d81a` *before* the merge so the smoke could run, so its build **predates the squash** even though the tree is identical. Saying "both post-squash" would have been false, and this file has been wrong about deploy state three times already.

Production was deliberately **not** deployed pre-merge: the change is breaking, the additive carve-out doesn't cover it, and deploying early would have put production on code absent from `main`.

⚠ **The Rule 21 backstop is empty, not merely degraded.** CodeRabbit is on the Free plan (summary only) and Gemini sunset 2026-07-17. So a rewrite of the contract governing numbers in agents' reports received **zero line-by-line review from either**. Restore one before C1 — that is the slice that starts sending real calls.

Next: **slice C1** in `C:\Projects\kqm-crm`. Its first task is a stop condition — `crm.activities` has no campaign column, so establish whether `session_id` or `opportunity_id` reliably carries it before writing anything.

## Archived 2026-09-02 (post-#935 fill - Rule 16 caps)

### Last updated entry (dropped per the 3-entry cap)

**Prior:** **PR #927 merged to `main` (squash `7e094f11`, 2026-08-27) - Daily Capture stops overwriting the calls KQM logged while it was open. Slice D.** The daily doc has TWO WRITERS and they write in different ways: Daily Capture holds ABSOLUTE values and saved the WHOLE form through `setDoc(..., {merge:true})`, while `ingestCallActivity` writes INCREMENTS all day with nobody watching. **`merge:true` does not merge numbers** - an ABSENT key is left alone, a PRESENT one overwrites, and every field was always present. So an agent who opened Daily Capture at 09:00, made thirty calls in KQM and pressed Save at 17:00 wrote `dials: 0` over all thirty, from form state loaded before any of them existed - no error, nothing logged, and the daily doc looks perfectly plausible afterwards. **Eight fields collide:** `dials`, `dialsByType`, `telContacts`, `serviceCalls`, `serviceContacts`, `appointmentsSet`, `newNamesAdded`, `ffisScheduled`. **Latent until now only because nothing incremented daily docs yet** - slice C1's sweep turns that on, which is why this landed first. **The fix uses `merge:true`'s asymmetry rather than fighting it:** every setter marks its top-level key dirty and the save sends only those keys, so a field the agent never touched is left OUT of the write entirely and whatever the ingest put there survives - the common case closes with no prompt at all. **It cannot close the case where the agent TYPES into a colliding field**, because there the two writers genuinely disagree and only a person can settle it: the save re-reads immediately before writing and, if such a field moved since the form loaded, STOPS and offers `Keep the KQM numbers` / `Use mine instead`. That remaining hole is stated at the top of `dailyPatch.js` rather than left for a reader to discover. `dialsByType` is never writable by the form - no input writes it, and writing the form's zeroes over the ingest's split would flatten the partition `outcomeMap.js` property-tests. **The earlier framing of slice D was WRONG and is corrected here:** it was banked as "no `serviceCalls` input in Daily Capture", which is the SMALLER half - that gap zeroes a DRAFT only (`sundayDailyToWeekly` skips submitted weeks) and closes itself the moment C1 turns on, exactly as the retired-guard comment anticipated. The collision is the real risk and it is a different fix. **Three existing tests failed, correctly** - they asserted the write-everything behaviour. Rather than weaken them, the key-name guards moved onto `createEmptyDailyEntry`, where the names are actually defined (strictly stronger: it catches a stale-cased key whether or not any UI happens to write it), and a NEW assertion checks the ingest-owned fields do NOT ride along untouched - the one that would have caught this bug. The S1 Retry contract now counts one `getDailyEntry` per save attempt, the pre-save re-read. **Drift guard:** `dailyPatch.test.js` `createRequire`s the CJS `functions/callActivity/outcomeMap.js` and pins the field list against its DERIVED `WRITABLE_FIELDS`, so an effect added there without a change here fails the suite instead of that field silently losing its ingested value again. eslint clean on all four files; daily + schema suites 260 passed; **full suite 383 files / 6097 tests, all green**. One earlier full run showed a single `activity-code-twin-guard` failure - it was **two overlapping vitest processes**, not this change: the guard passes in isolation (5/5) and in the clean full run. **No deploy-gated surfaces touched** - `src/` only, confirmed with `git show --stat` - so it ships with the Vercel rebuild at merge, the same not-gated path PR #923's aggregator half took.

### Where-we-left-off entry (dropped per the 3-entry cap)

**PRIOR - 2026-08-27. PR #927 merged to `main` (squash `7e094f11`) - Daily Capture stops overwriting the calls KQM logged while it was open. Slice D. MERGED; NO DEPLOY-GATED SURFACE.**

I told the operator slice D was urgent because of `serviceCalls`. Reading the code showed I had the emphasis wrong, and the correction is the useful part of this cycle.

- **The `serviceCalls` gap is real but narrow.** The aggregator's omit-when-zero guard was retired in slice B *in anticipation* of KQM being its daily writer. KQM is not live yet, so the aggregator writes `serviceCalls: 0` over anything typed on the weekly wizard - but only into a **draft**, because `sundayDailyToWeekly` skips submitted reports. It closes itself the moment C1 turns on, exactly as that comment anticipated.
- **The real problem is a write collision that C1 CREATES.** `saveDailyEntry` does `setDoc(..., { merge: true })` with every field always present, and `DailyCaptureV2` loads the doc into form state on mount. Eight fields are both typed inputs there and `FieldValue.increment` targets in `ingestCallActivity`. An agent who opens the form in the morning, dials a KQM block all day and saves in the evening overwrites every call ingested in between - from stale form state, with no error anywhere.

**The operator chose re-read-and-reconcile** over splitting field ownership into a `fromCalls` sub-map. What shipped:

- `src/lib/schema/dailyPatch.js` - pure. `buildDailyPatch` sends only touched keys; `detectConflicts` compares the values at form load against a re-read taken immediately before writing.
- `DailyCaptureV2.jsx` - dirty tracking on every setter, baseline captured at load, pre-save re-read, and a conflict banner offering `Keep the KQM numbers` (drops the field from the write, so merge:true preserves the ingest) or `Use mine instead`.
- 15 new pure tests and 5 new component tests.

**Two things worth carrying forward.**

**The three failing tests were right, and rewriting them was the work.** They asserted the write-everything behaviour - the exact bug. The key-name guards moved onto `createEmptyDailyEntry`, which is where the names are defined and catches a stale-cased key whether or not any UI writes it; a new assertion pins that the ingest-owned fields must NOT ride along untouched. Deleting them would have removed a real guard; leaving them would have pinned the bug.

**A single full-suite failure was mine, but not my code's.** `activity-code-twin-guard` failed once with an offender list that never printed. The guard passes in isolation (5/5) and in a clean full run of 383 files / 6097 tests. The cause: the device bridge timed out on a long run, left its vitest workers alive, and the next run raced them. **Worth remembering - a bridge timeout does not kill the process it started.**

**NEXT: C1's three operator steps**, each needing a credential I should not hold - mint a call source per agent, set the Edge Function secrets (**staging first**), `create extension pg_cron` plus the schedule. Runbook: `kqm-crm/docs/agencytrack-dispatch-setup.md`.

The guard is the reason it was a red build instead of a wrong number in an agent's report. **`mapEffects` gained a variable increment** - `typeof row.amount === 'function' ? row.amount(effects) : 1` - so a row can move money rather than a count, which #931 then used. **In KQM the TRIGGER was the real gate, not the projection:** `trg_activity_to_agencytrack` fired `WHEN (new.kind = 'call')`, so widening `crm.agencytrack_effect_payload()` alone would have looked like a working migration while every meeting and milestone silently never enqueued. `sql/37_agencytrack_ladder.sql` widens both. Applied to Supabase `oujnscwyxwuzmgyszkxd`. **DEPLOYED to both projects** (see #931's row - the two deployed together; neither is meaningful alone). |

### Where-we-left-off entry (dropped per the 3-entry cap)

**PRIOR - 2026-08-30. Consolidated fill for PRs #929-#933 - the selling ladder ships end to end, and the medical limits become data. Ladder DEPLOYED to both projects; #933 has no deploy-gated surface.**

Five merges, one fill (Rule 16(c)). The ladder half is the one that changes what a manager sees; the medical-limits half is the one that changes what an agent can honestly offer a client in the room.

- **The ladder (#929, #931).** `kind` plus six flags - `approachHeld`, `presented`, `ffiHeld`, `closingHeld`, `policyDelivered`, `appSubmitted` - and `apiAmount`. KQM normalises, AgencyTrack validates, and the pairing rules are 400s in both directions rather than a generous reading. `sql/37` and `sql/38` are applied against Supabase `oujnscwyxwuzmgyszkxd`.
- **Two defects, and neither was found by reading the diff.** CI reddened on #929's drift guard - `INGEST_WRITTEN_FIELDS` had not gained the five ladder fields - which is precisely the overwrite slice D was built to stop, arriving the first time the vocabulary grew. The second had no guard at all: `newBusiness` is a nested map, `detectConflicts` compared it whole, and numeric coercion made both sides `0`, so **no map field could ever raise the conflict banner**. Every defect in this estate fails closed and quietly; the ones that got caught got caught by making the arithmetic run.
- **In KQM the trigger, not the projection, was the gate.** `WHEN (new.kind = 'call')` would have left every meeting and milestone silently un-enqueued behind a migration that looked like it worked.
- **I named an event wrong and the operator corrected it.** "Application taken" is not a business event: an agent **writes** an application, the CRO **submits** it weekly, and awards run on **settled** business. Three events, one word, mine. Renamed to `Application submitted`, with `Policy delivered` beside it. I also had the clawback clock backwards - issue precedes dispatch, so `dateIssued + 30` warns too early.
- **The medical limits (#933).** Encoded as effective-dated data with `requirementsFor` / `headroomFor` on top, mirrored in the planning app. The age-band mapping was reconstructed from PDF layout and then **confirmed against the printed table by the operator**; `bandMappingConfirmed` records that so an unchecked future transcription looks different. **"Non-Medical" is a form - life application part 2** - not an absent requirement, and the label now says so wherever it renders.
- **A worked example of mine was false and the tests said so.** A client sitting exactly at a tier ceiling has spent their evidence, not banked spare. Headroom exists strictly *inside* a tier.

**What is next, and the first item is not mine to do:** the operator logs one real `Application submitted` through the KQM Person page to exercise the money path end to end. Then `ingestProspect` (brief H), which is unbuilt while prospect rows queue up `pending`. The life pipeline (`claude/life-pipeline-brief.md`) is briefed in four phases and not started - and most of what it describes already exists in AgencyTrack, which is the surprise worth carrying forward. **Open and dated: age basis** on the medical table (a one-year error at every band boundary), the 25 Aug call that predates the trigger and will not self-heal, an empty Rule 21 backstop, and **Node 20's decommission on 2026-10-30**.

---

| | [#933](https://github.com/Kelsean868/agencytrack/pull/933) | `7d3913e3` | **feat(underwriting): Tatil's revised medical limits as data, with the headroom derivation.** The `ProductCatalog` spec has always listed *non-medical limits* among the things it should hold; they existed nowhere - not here, not in the planning app, not in the rater portal. **The headline: the non-medical limit is 500,000 for ages 0-50** (children stretch to 750,000 with HIV + APS), **there is NO non-medical band from 51** - paramedical from the first dollar - and over 60 a full Medical from the first dollar. A small policy for a 52-year-old is not a quick policy. **"Non-Medical" is a FORM, not the absence of a requirement** - it is life application part 2, which the agent completes with the client - so `EXAM_LEVEL_LABELS` carries "Non-Medical form (life application part 2)" and a test forbids the bare token reaching a UI, because to an agent, and worse to a client, it reads as *no medical requirement*. **The quiet rule, verbatim typo included:** "A MEDICAL IS REQUIRED FOR APPLICATIONS WITH DISIBILITY INCOME RIDER FOR ALL SUMS ASSURED" - a 100,000 case that is otherwise non-medical becomes a Medical the moment a DIR is attached. **`headroomFor` is deliberately NOT `tierCeiling - issued`**, which is wrong in the direction that embarrasses an agent in front of a client: 1,500,000 pulls in a financial statement and an inspection report whatever the tier, 3,000,000 adds a urine screen for ages 16-60 only, and from 5,000,000 the document refuses to specify. A 35-year-old issued 1,400,000 has **99,999** of free headroom, not the 600,000 the tier suggests; `free` is true only when the client would do nothing at all, and anything beyond comes back separately as `nextStep`. **Two tests caught my own error:** I had written that a 55-year-old issued 750,000 runs to 2,000,000 on the same evidence. They do not - 500,001-750,000 asks for a *fasting lipid* profile, 750,001-2,000,000 a *complete* one. **Sitting exactly at a tier ceiling means the evidence is spent, not spare**, and the child-at-500,000 case is the same trap. **Effective-dated by construction:** the file is named for its effective date, these changed on 1 April alongside the LifeSpan Gold rates, and the memo is explicit that a case follows the rules in force when it was received AND submitted - so `limitsInForceOn()` returns **null** before the earliest table rather than falling back to the newest, and a revision is a new file beside this one. **The age-band mapping was reconstructed from PDF layout** (the text layer emits its five age headings after their five blocks) **and then CONFIRMED against the printed table by the operator, 30 Aug** - `bandMappingConfirmed` records the check, and three tests pin the confirmed facts so a future unchecked transcription is visibly different. **Still open: age basis** - whether Tatil reckons age next birthday, a one-year error at every boundary. Mirrored in `Financial-Planning-App/parameters/tt-parameters.json` (PR #2 there, merged); both sides assert the same key figures but **no test inside either repo can read the other**, so it is a handshake, not enforcement, and both files say so. `src/`-only: no deploy-gated surface. | |

| | [#931](https://github.com/Kelsean868/agencytrack/pull/931) | `b73a0bb8` | **feat(call-activity): a submitted application may move production credit.** The ladder's top rung, and the first time KQM can move MONEY rather than counts: `appSubmitted` plus `apiAmount`, paired in BOTH directions by `coherenceError` - an amount without the flag and a flag without an amount are each a 400, along with non-finite, negative and over-cap (1,000,000) values. **The naming defect this fixes was mine, and the operator caught it.** I shipped "Application taken", which is not what the business says: an agent logs an application when it is **written**, the CRO **submits** it later in a weekly batch, and awards and Agent of the Month run on **settled** business - three different events I had collapsed into one word. `Application submitted` is the event; `Policy delivered` was added beside it, because issue and delivery are different dates and the 30-day clawback clock hangs off the second. **The nested-map hazard had no guard and could not have raised one:** `newBusiness` is a MAP, `detectConflicts` compared it whole, and numeric coercion turned both sides into `0` - so **the conflict banner was unreachable for every map field**, silently. Fixed leaf-wise via `INGEST_WRITTEN_LEAVES` and `leafPathsOf`, with `DailyCaptureV2` marking `newBusiness.apps` dirty rather than `newBusiness`. **`setDoc merge:true` merges nested maps leaf-wise** - that asymmetry is the whole mechanism, the same one slice D used. **Awards needed no change and that was checked, not assumed:** `awardsEngine.js` already separates settled (`confirmedData`) from submitted (`'estimated'`), so feeding submitted API from KQM cannot inflate a settled award. KQM half: `sql/38_submitted_and_delivered.sql`, applied - and it re-points ApplyOn with a **guarded surgical `DO` block** rather than retyping ~100 live vocabulary rows. **`apply_outcome_meta`'s guard key moved again** (`kept` -> `submitted`); it must move to the newest flag every time the vocabulary grows, which is invisible and will recur. **DEPLOYED to both projects, queried:** production ACTIVE `2026-08-30T14:09:36.981Z`, staging `14:06:39.770Z`, both post-squash. | |

### Last updated entry (dropped per the 3-entry cap)

**Prior:** **PR #934 merged to `main` (squash `c18c787f`, 2026-08-30) - the medical limits are read on AGE NEXT BIRTHDAY. MERGED; NO DEPLOY-GATED SURFACE.** #933 shipped the table with one question flagged as the expensive one, and the operator answered it: **Tatil reckons age next birthday, for premiums and for these medical requirements alike.** ANB is always attained age **+ 1** - a constant offset, not a mid-year switch - so it moves every band boundary by a year. **One of those boundaries is not like the others, and it is the whole reason this could not stay a comment:** a client whose ATTAINED age is 50 is underwritten at **51**, and **at 51 there is no non-medical band at all**. An agent reading the table off attained age tells them "non-medical up to 500,000" and then sends them for a paramedical nobody warned them about - same client, same cover, one day of their life apart, two different conversations. A test asserts exactly that divergence rather than describing it. **`ageNextBirthday(dateOfBirth, asOf)` is pure and `asOf` is REQUIRED, not defaulted** - the module has no clock by design, and Trinidad is UTC-4 with no DST, so a UTC "today" is the previous calendar day for four hours every evening, which at a birthday boundary is a wrong band; callers pass `getTodayTT()` from `utils/dateInputs`. It returns **null** rather than guessing on a bad format, an impossible date (`2026-02-30`, which `Date` would otherwise roll silently into March) or an `asOf` before the birth. **`requirementsFor` / `headroomFor` now accept `dateOfBirth` + `asOf` and derive the age themselves.** A pre-computed `age` still works and means "this is already ANB", but **passing BOTH is a hard refusal rather than a silent preference** - two sources of truth for the age is how a caller ends up trusting the wrong one. Both functions echo back the `age` they used, so a UI can show the client which age the answer was reckoned at instead of leaving them to wonder why 50 reads as 51. **One judgement call, written down rather than buried:** a client born 29 February has no birthday in a common year, and the code treats **1 March** as the day their age steps. That is a decision, not a fact from the document - it affects one day a year for one cohort, and if Tatil's own system steps on 28 February that cohort is a year out for that day. It is stated in the function, in its test and in the project doc so it can be overturned deliberately. `ageBasis` on the table is now `'age next birthday (attained age + 1)'` with `ageBasisConfirmed: '2026-08-30'`, replacing the "confirm this before deriving an age" caveat #933 carried. **Mutation-verified:** replacing `attained + 1` with `attained` - the exact defect of reading the table on attained age - reds **8 tests and nothing else**, including all four 50/51 boundary cases; restored, 46/46 green, eslint clean. **Twin merged:** Financial-Planning-App PR #3 (`7949d9d`) carries the same helper in `parameters/tt-parameters.js` and asserts the same 50/51 pair against its own JSON bands in `verify.mjs`. `src/`-only, so no deploy-gated surface: it ships with the Vercel rebuild at merge.

### Where-we-left-off entry (dropped per the 3-entry cap)

**PRIOR - 2026-08-30. PR #934 merged to `main` (squash `c18c787f`) - the medical limits are read on age next birthday. MERGED; NO DEPLOY-GATED SURFACE.**

#933 encoded the table and flagged one thing as the expensive open question. The operator settled it in a sentence: Tatil reckons **age next birthday**, for premiums and for the medical requirements alike.

- **ANB is attained age + 1, always.** A constant offset, not a mid-year switch. That makes it easy to implement and easy to get catastrophically wrong by ignoring.
- **The 50/51 boundary is the reason this needed code.** A client whose attained age is 50 is underwritten at 51 - and at 51 the non-medical band does not exist. Quote them off attained age and you have promised a non-medical case and booked a paramedical. The suite asserts that divergence rather than describing it.
- **`asOf` is required, not defaulted.** The module is pure and has no clock. Trinidad is UTC-4 with no DST, so a UTC "today" is the previous calendar day for four hours every evening - at a birthday boundary, a wrong band. The estate has been bitten by this before; this is the same trap in a new place.
- **Both-age-and-dateOfBirth is refused, not resolved.** A caller with two sources of truth for the age will eventually trust the wrong one, and a function that quietly picks is what lets them.

**The one open judgement call is the leap day.** A client born 29 February has no birthday in a common year; the code steps their age on 1 March. That is a choice, not something the document says - written into the function, its test and the project doc so it can be overturned deliberately rather than discovered.

**What is next is unchanged, and the first item is not mine to do:** the operator logs one real `Application submitted` through the KQM Person page to exercise the money path end to end. Then `ingestProspect` (brief H), unbuilt while prospect rows queue up `pending`. The life pipeline is briefed in four phases and not started. **Dated risk: Node 20 decommissions 2026-10-30.**

### Last updated entry (dropped per the 3-entry cap)

**Prior:** **PR #935 merged to `main` (squash `332b63b2`, 2026-09-02) - a policy record opens at `written`. MERGED; `firestore.rules` DEPLOYED TO BOTH PROJECTS AND QUERIED.** Life-pipeline phase 1, slice 1A (D1). A policy record opens when the application is WRITTEN, not when it is submitted - which is what gives an agent the "waiting on signature" list they do not have today, and what phase 1.5 will hang the KQM join on, since today the ledger record and the KQM event are created by two different people at two different moments with nothing linking them. **`POLICY_STATUSES` gains `written` at the head and `LEGAL_AGENT_TRANSITIONS.written` is `['submitted', 'ntu']` only** - an application written and then abandoned is an NTU, not a denial, because nobody underwrote it. **`dateSubmitted` is required on the `written -> submitted` EDGE, never on the `submitted` TARGET**, and that distinction is the whole design: `submitted` is ALSO the target of the field-free `postponed -> submitted` re-entry, so widening `TRANSITION_REQUIRED_FIELDS.submitted` would have broken the re-entry the moment it shipped. New `EDGE_REQUIRED_FIELDS` keyed `${from}->${to}` plus `requiredFieldsForTransition(from, to)` on the JS side; the rules branch Arm B on `resource.data.status` rather than on the target. Two regression guards now stand on it: the emulator's `postponed -> submitted (no new fields) -> ALLOW` case, and a service test asserting the re-entry writes NO `dateSubmitted`. **A SIXTH pipeline tile, `Written`, at the head - this DELIBERATELY REVISES the Rule 9 banked decision that locks 5 tiles**, and the PR body says so rather than folding it silently: folding `written` into Submitted would hide exactly the list the status exists to create, the applications sitting unsigned. The same reasoning grew the drill drawer's lifecycle bar from four nodes to five - without it a policy at `written` renders with "Submitted" as its current node, which is false on screen and not merely untidy. **No migration, and no script either** - every existing policy is at `submitted` or beyond and `written` has no inbound edge, so there are no documents to change; stated in the PR rather than shipping a no-op. **The additive carve-out did NOT apply**: Arm A's status guard WIDENS from `== 'submitted'` to `in ['written', 'submitted']`, a behaviour change to an existing arm, so merging shipped code and the rules were released after it. **CI caught the one thing the pre-commit sweep missed, and it is worth writing down:** `policiesService.test.js` asserts the create payload's status by LITERAL VALUE and imports none of the changed symbols, so a symbol-based grep could not see it. Fixing it exposed a real defect in NEW code, not only a stale test - `toTimestamp` detected an existing Timestamp by duck-typing `toMillis` and then called `.toMillis()` to compare, both assumptions false against a test double that has neither and neither of them necessary; comparison now reads milliseconds from the RAW input via `millisOf`, which accepts `toMillis()`, `toDate()`, `.seconds`, a `Date` or a `"YYYY-MM-DD"` string and returns `NaN` rather than a garbage number. `tests/rules/policies.rules.test.mjs` **97/97** with 12 new cases, pasted verbatim in the PR body; full vitest **384/384 files, 6,181 tests**; eslint clean; `npm run build` succeeds.

### Active track entry (dropped per the 3-entry cap)

**Prior:** **Life-pipeline phase 1 is OPEN, and slice 1A is CLOSED (PR #935, `332b63b2`; rules deployed and queried on both projects).** The brief is `docs/briefs/life-pipeline-phase1-custody-and-clock.md`; decisions D1-D6 are operator-approved and are not re-opened. **NEXT is slice 1B, the four custody dates** - `dispatchedAt`, `branchNotifiedAt`, `agentCollectedAt` as nullable timestamps on a policy that stays `settled` throughout (D2: the custody chain is DATES, not statuses, because ten transitions to police in the rules is really one linear walk and it breaks the first time a package skips a step), a `recordCustodyDates` service function mirroring `transitionPolicyStatus`'s `writeBatch` so the walk with money on it is as auditable as a status change, and **rules Arm F - the first agent-writable arm on a `settled` policy, and the tightest arm in the file.** Today no agent arm can touch a settled policy at all, and that is a property worth losing carefully. Its stop condition is explicit in the brief: **if `hasOnly` cannot be written so that it provably excludes every money field, STOP and surface rather than deciding alone.** Backdating is allowed and future dates never are; every date must be `>= dateIssued`, because a dispatch date before the issue date is a typo that would move the deadline EARLIER, the direction that costs an agent commission. Ordering (`dispatchedAt <= branchNotifiedAt <= agentCollectedAt`) is validated in the SERVICE and the UI, never in the rules - rules enforce the safety property, ordering is a data-quality nicety and a real package can teach the agent two dates out of order. **1B carries a named mutation ritual:** delete the `hasOnly` clause, re-run, and report observed counts BOTH ways - the forbidden-field tests must go red and nothing else. Then 1C (re-anchor `clawbackClock.js` to dispatch, Sonnet 5 / medium), 1D (held, not lost - the clawback is HELD and delivery releases it, Sonnet 5 / medium) and 1E (the branch view, Sonnet 5 / medium). **One process lesson from 1A, banked because it will recur:** grep for the LITERAL VALUES a change touches, not only for the symbols - `policiesService.test.js` asserted `status === 'submitted'` at create and imports none of the changed constants, so it was invisible to a symbol sweep and CI found it instead of the sweep.

### Where-we-left-off entry (dropped per the 3-entry cap)

**PRIOR - 2026-09-02. PR #935 merged to `main` (squash `332b63b2`) - a policy record opens at `written`. Life-pipeline phase 1, slice 1A. MERGED, AND THE RULES ARE DEPLOYED AND QUERIED ON BOTH PROJECTS.**

The brief (`docs/briefs/life-pipeline-phase1-custody-and-clock.md`) opens by saying this is the only phase where the app is currently *wrong* rather than merely incomplete. 1A is the half that makes the record exist at the right moment: a policy opens when the application is WRITTEN, not when it is submitted.

- **Why it earns its place.** Without `written` the KQM event and the ledger record are created by two different people at two different moments, and there is nothing for phase 1.5's join to hang on. It also gives an agent a list they simply do not have today - the applications sitting unsigned.
- **`written -> ntu` is legal; `written -> denied` is not.** An application written and then abandoned is an NTU. Nobody underwrote it, so nobody denied it. That is a small distinction that keeps the underwriting statistics honest.
- **The per-EDGE field requirement is the design, not a detail.** `dateSubmitted` is required on `written -> submitted` and nowhere else. `submitted` is also the target of the field-free `postponed -> submitted` re-entry, so a per-TARGET encoding would have broken the re-entry the moment it shipped. The rules branch Arm B on `resource.data.status`; the JS side gets `EDGE_REQUIRED_FIELDS` and `requiredFieldsForTransition`. Two guards protect it - the emulator's re-entry case and a service test asserting the re-entry writes no `dateSubmitted`.
- **The Rule 9 5-tile decision is deliberately revised, in writing.** Six tiles now, `Written` at the head. Folding it into Submitted would hide exactly the list the status exists to create, and a tile an agent cannot see is a status nobody keeps current. The drill drawer's lifecycle bar grew from four nodes to five for the same reason: without it a `written` policy renders with "Submitted" as its current node, which is simply false on screen.
- **No migration, and no script either.** Every existing policy is at `submitted` or beyond and `written` has no inbound edge, so there is nothing to migrate. Stated in the PR rather than shipping a no-op.

**The rules were deploy-gated and the carve-out did NOT apply.** Arm A's status guard widens from `== 'submitted'` to `in ['written', 'submitted']` - a behaviour change to an existing arm, not an additive one - so merging shipped code, and the rules were released afterwards. Staging first, then production. **The deploy state was QUERIED, not inferred from a clean CLI exit:** the released ruleset was read back from the Firebase Rules API on both projects and compared byte-for-byte with `firestore.rules` at `332b63b2`, and the three new guards were asserted present in the live source. Production ruleset `c91970e6-5428-440f-b76e-2ff6ca6c04cf` at `2026-09-02T15:06:12.256382Z`; staging `63623d5b-5b6f-4db2-b03f-973d04e325d7` at `2026-09-02T15:04:44.324835Z`.

**CI found what the pre-commit sweep could not, and the reason generalises.** `policiesService.test.js` asserted the create payload's status by LITERAL VALUE and imports none of the changed symbols, so a sweep that grepped for importers of `POLICY_STATUSES` / `pipelineStage` / the transition maps was structurally blind to it. **Next time: grep for the literal values a change touches, not only the symbols.** Fixing it surfaced a genuine defect in new code rather than only a stale expectation - `toTimestamp` identified an existing Timestamp by duck-typing `toMillis` and then called `.toMillis()` to compare, both assumptions false against a test double that has neither, and neither of them necessary. Comparison now reads milliseconds from the RAW input through `millisOf` and returns `NaN` rather than a garbage number when it cannot read one.

**Verification, all of it re-run rather than reported:** `tests/rules/policies.rules.test.mjs` 97/97 with 12 new `1A:` cases, pasted verbatim into the PR body; full vitest 384/384 files and 6,181 tests; `eslint .` clean; `npm run build` green. The three JSX suites were declared a gap in the PR body when they had only been read - that gap is now closed, they are run and they pass.

**What is next: slice 1B, the four custody dates**, and it is the slice that opens the first agent-writable arm on a `settled` policy. Arm F is the tightest arm in the file and carries an explicit stop condition - if `hasOnly` cannot be written so it provably excludes every money field, stop and surface. It also carries a mutation ritual: delete the `hasOnly` clause, re-run, and report observed counts both ways. **Still open and unchanged from before this cycle:** the operator logs one real `Application submitted` through the KQM Person page to exercise the money path end to end; `ingestProspect` (brief H) is unbuilt while prospect rows queue `pending`; the 25 Aug call predates the trigger and will not self-heal; the Rule 21 backstop is still EMPTY; and **Node 20 decommissions 2026-10-30**, untouched by this phase but not moved by it either.

### Where-we-left-off entry (PR #936 medical-limits-September block, dropped when #941 became the 4th entry)

**PRIOR - 2026-09-03. PR #936 merged to `main` (squash `5cb6b5f3`) - medical limits move to the September 2026 schedule. MERGED; NO DEPLOY-GATED SURFACE.**

Slice M1 of a two-repo brief (`docs/briefs/medical-limits-september-kickoff.md`). The Tatil Life Underwriting Requirements Schedule changed effective 1 September 2026, and this is the second table the April build (#933/#934) was shaped to receive - a new file beside the old one, `2026-09.js` beside `2026-04.js`, never an edit to it.

- **The schedule moved further than the memo.** The memo's own summary table names three changes; the schedule carries nine. Per the brief, the schedule is what is encoded, and the gap is written into the PR body rather than reconciled quietly.
- **The non-medical ceiling for 16-50 triples, and 51-60 gains a non-medical band it never had.** 500,000 to 1,500,000 for the working ages; 51-60 goes from paramedical-at-the-first-dollar to a real non-medical tier at 500,000. The paramedical tier that used to sit between 16 and 60 is gone entirely - one is left, in 0-15, which is why `Paramedical` stays in `EXAM_LEVELS`.
- **The panels became data because their contents changed.** April flattened each blood panel's composition into the tier string; September names both panels in footnotes, and the lipid panel LOST VLDL between the two dates - so the composition now lives beside the date it was true, in `bloodPanels`, keyed by the page's own token.
- **Aggregation is new, and it is one input, not two.** The schedule states the tier is picked on the total risk amount - the new application plus everything issued or reinstated on the same life in the trailing 12 months. `otherCoverLast12Months` carries it; every answer echoes `assessedAmount` and `assumesNoOtherCover`, and OMITTED is deliberately not the same as ZERO - omitted means nobody looked. No `termRiderAmount`: a second field is how two callers fold the same rider in twice.
- **`CURRENT_MEDICAL_LIMITS` now points at September**, and the flip has NO caller today - verified with a repo-wide grep before merging, so nothing user-facing changed.

**Two things worth carrying forward.**

**CI's one failure was the documented flake, and the proof was already written down before this PR needed it.** `DailyCaptureV2.test.jsx:254` failed on first push, an exact match for the A2 assertion-shape family `docs/FOLLOW_UPS.md` already names and already prescribes the fix for - a test this PR does not touch, with no import path from its diff. Re-ran with zero code change; green.

**The smoke was refused, not skipped, and the reason is worth carrying past this PR.** A FortiGate on the operator's network TLS-intercepts `*.vercel.app` specifically - verified against `portal.agencytrack.app`, `github.com` and `google.com`, all untouched on the same machine, same minute. Forcing the bypass token through it would mean disabling certificate verification to push a live credential across a connection known to be decrypted and re-signed; that was refused as a workaround rather than attempted. It would not have proven anything about this change anyway - `dist/assets` grep confirms the whole module is tree-shaken out of the production bundle, so zero bytes of this PR reach a browser today.

**What is next: Slice M2, in `Financial-Planning-App`, not this repo.** It restructures `parameters/tt-parameters.json` to carry both tables (`medical_limits` current, `medical_limits_superseded` array) and copies the September token names this slice just settled. Run separately, after this merge, per the brief. **Also open:** the band mapping wants an operator eyes-on check against the printed schedule (stated as a known gap, not hidden); nothing in `src/` yet consumes `requirementsFor`/`headroomFor` - the headroom offer is the first real consumer; and the Fortinet TLS-interception finding is its own item - if longstanding, `VERCEL_BYPASS_TOKEN` has already crossed it on every past preview smoke from this machine and may be worth rotating.

### Where-we-left-off entry (PRs #937/#938 block, dropped when #942 became the 4th entry)

**PRIOR - 2026-09-08. PRs #937 (`a85d1400`, 2026-09-06) and #938 (`9f619a29`, 2026-09-08) merged to `main` - Tatil's 24-month persistency model, then the same-day follow-up that makes the new month reachable. MERGED; `firestore.rules` DEPLOYED TO BOTH PROJECTS AND QUERIED (from #937); #938 has NO DEPLOY-GATED SURFACE.**

Slice P1 of `docs/briefs/persistency-24-month-model-brief.md`, ratifying the Tatil Life memo of 29 Aug 2026 (effective September 2026 onward). Persistency months from `2026-09` compute over a 24-month window instead of 12, and gain a seventh input, `Decreases`, subtracted alongside `Not Takens` in the Net Gross Settled figure that every award gate reads.

- **#937 built the formula and the guard.** `persistencyModelFor(monthKey)` (new, `src/lib/persistency/model.js`) is the sole authority for `legacy12` vs `tatil24`, pure, and throws on a malformed key rather than silently defaulting - a silent fallback would report a month on the wrong model and render a plausible wrong number on a surface that gates awards. `calculateGrossSettled` defaults `decreases` to `0`, so every pre-September document derives byte-identically to what it always did - proved on a six-input Feb 2026 regression fixture, before and after, identical to four decimal places. `firestore.rules` gained one additive guard - `decreases` absent OR `>= 0` - verified against the `/persistency` match block's absence of `hasOnly`, so pre-September writes validate exactly as before (emulator case 21 pins it).
- **#937 also surfaced, in its own PR body rather than fixing it inline, that nobody could reach the new month.** `getAvailableMonths` only listed months with an existing document and fell back to "today" only when the tenant held zero records anywhere - a pre-existing month-selector gap, outside P1's file inventory, that the memo made acute and dated. The PR recommended a small follow-up.
- **#938 (P1b) closed that gap the same day.** The picker now unions doc-derived months with an always-on three-month window - the current TT month plus the two before it, derived from `getTodayTT()` rather than `new Date()`, because a UTC "today" is the previous calendar day for four hours every Trinidad evening. `savePersistency` now refuses a save when the derived Net Gross Settled is negative - Decreases bigger than Business Placed is a transcription error, not a real state - while zero (an agent with no business in the window) stays saveable. The entry form's hardcoded `0.90`/"90%" gate literal became `PERS_GATE`/`PERS_GATE_PCT` from the shared module.
- **Confirmed on the preview, not asserted:** the manager month picker now reads `2026-09, 2026-08, 2026-07, 2026-06`. Before #938, only whatever months already had documents were offered, and September had none.
- **Deploy state was QUERIED during this fill, not inferred from a clean CLI exit.** The Firebase Rules API was read directly (no `firebase deploy` was run): production `agencytrack-2a610` ruleset `d722881c-b4bf-4deb-8521-c42deab74122` released `2026-09-06T23:43:30Z`, eight minutes after #937's `23:35:10Z` merge; staging `agencytrack-staging` ruleset `da22dcda-76fa-4974-b625-2b2a3dc091bf` released `23:41:25Z`, two minutes earlier - staging first, same pattern as #935. Both were downloaded and diffed byte-for-byte against `firestore.rules` at the current main HEAD: identical. #938 alone touches no `firestore.rules`, `functions/`, or `firestore.indexes.json` (`git diff --stat a85d1400 9f619a29` confirms it), so it ships with the Vercel rebuild and nothing further to deploy.
- **The Rule 21 backstop was empty across both PRs**, and it is now two consecutive PRs on this repo with zero Gemini activity - worth investigating as its own item rather than only re-noting. CodeRabbit's Free plan gave summary-only reviews on both; no bot read the money-adjacent `savePersistency` negative-denominator guard line-by-line.

**What is next:** slice P2 of the parent brief - the vocabulary sweep across readers/CSV headers and retiring the `persistencyV2` preview shell (`rollingModelV2.js`, `PersistencyV2Shell.jsx`, the `persistencyV2` flag). Two corrections are already banked against it so they aren't re-discovered at dispatch: `companyConfigRegistry.js` does not cite `calculations.js` by line number (P2 item 4 is void), and the "agent self-entry form" / "manager entry form" the parent brief describes as two components are actually one (`PersistencyEntryForm.jsx`) - one edit, not two. Slice P3 (the per-policy lapse engine) stays gated behind a Tatil process document that does not exist yet. **Unchanged and carried:** life-pipeline slice 1B (the four custody dates, rules Arm F) is still next on that track; Node 20 decommissions 2026-10-30.
